# Opening book source: annotated 5dpgn lines. `ktools bookgen data/book_theory_src.txt >
# data/book_theory.txt` turns it into the book the engine loads (a test checks the two agree).
#
# One game per non-comment line (a 5dpgn header and movetext). Each action may carry a tag in
# a comment right after it:
#   {play N}  the engine may play it, chosen at random with weight N among the position's
#             play moves (10: the guide's recommendation, 5: a good alternative, 2-3: a
#             playable sideline);
#   {know}    known theory the engine must not play itself (a blunder, a move the guide calls
#             dubious or not recommended, or the losing side's moves in a trap), kept so the
#             positions after it, and our reply there, are in the book.
# An untagged action takes the tag given to the same action from the same position elsewhere
# in the file (every action must be tagged somewhere; conflicting tags are an error).
#
# Sources (docs/theory/): t0_opening_guide.txt (Theory Gang, "T0 Openings Guide" v2.0, with its
# line numbers below), t0_common_openings.txt, f7_sac_guide_notes.md (Lavamat's f7-sac guide).
# Fixes to the guide's lines are noted where made: a missing " / " between White's and Black's
# move, "(>1L)" for "(>L1)", and moves that contradict the guide's own prose.

# ============================================================================ Turn Zero
# --- Move 1 (t0_common_openings: "the best first move for both sides is ... Nf3 and Nf6"; the
# knight blocks the f-file travels and the h4/h5 queen sacs). Against any other first move
# Black still answers Nf6 for the same reason. Expert advice adds 1. e3 (a good alternative) and
# 1. e4 (sharp and risky) as White's other first moves, at lower weights.
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 {play 10} / Nf6 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e3 {play 5} / Nf6 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. d4 {know} / Nf6 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e4 {play 2} / Nf6 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. c4 {know} / Nf6 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. c3 {know} / Nf6 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nc3 {know} / Nf6 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. d3 {know} / Nf6 {play 10}

# --- Classical f7 defense, London structures (guide 107-146). "The most classical way ... to
# defend against the infamous f7"; 2. c4 (English) and 2. e3 are the other structures; the
# Mongolian 2. Nd4 is a trappy surprise weapon that "loses a lot of strength" against good
# replies, so we only answer it.
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 {play 10} / d5 {play 10}
# 3. c3 c6 "Recommended Structure" (113); 4. Bf4 "most commonly played" (119), Black mirrors.
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / d5 3. c3 {play 10} / c6 {play 10} 4. Bf4 {play 10} / Bf5 {play 10}
# 4. Bg5 Bg4 (122): active; after 4...h6 the bishop keeps the pin with Bh4, and after ...g5
# (which weakens Black's kingside) goes to g3 (prose of 123).
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / d5 3. c3 / c6 4. Bg5 {play 5} / Bg4 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / d5 3. c3 / c6 4. Bg5 / h6 {know} 5. Bh4 {play 10} / g5 {know} 6. Bg3 {play 10}
# 4. e3 e6 (128): "not recommended to play e3 before developing the queenside bishop" (both
# colours: the structures are symmetrical).
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / d5 3. c3 / c6 4. e3 {know} / e6 {know}
# 4. Nbd2 Nbd7 (131): "it's too slow".
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / d5 3. c3 / c6 4. Nbd2 {know} / Nbd7 {know}
# 4. Be3 Be6 (134): solid, little initiative.
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / d5 3. c3 / c6 4. Be3 {play 2} / Be6 {play 5}
# 3. Nc3 Nc6 (136): "this line is not recommended" (the knight is pinned through time).
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / d5 3. Nc3 {know} / Nc6 {know}
# 3. Bd2 (139): playable but careful after 3...c6, which sets the c3 trap: 4. c3/Nc3 are
# blunders to Qa5# (143; the prose says Qa4, the line and the board say Qa5); 4. e3 is the
# recommended reply (146). Black's 3...Bd7 mirror is not recommended to us.
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / d5 3. Bd2 {know} / Bd7 {know}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / d5 3. Bd2 / c6 {play 10} 4. Nc3 {know} / Qa5# {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / d5 3. Bd2 / c6 4. c3 {know} / Qa5# {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / d5 3. Bd2 / c6 4. e3 {play 10}

# --- Symmetrical English Four Knights (149-189): "solid and stable"; since guide v3.0 Black plays
# the asymmetrical 2...c6 / 2...e6 (see the v3.0 section at the end, lines 273-320), so 2...c5 is
# only a good alternative. Double fianchetto (153):
# "recommended for players of all levels".
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. c4 {play 5} / c5 {play 5} 3. Nc3 {play 10} / Nc6 {play 10} 4. g3 {play 10} / g6 {play 10} 5. Bg2 {play 10} / Bg7 {play 10}
# 4. e3 (157): flexible.
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. c4 / c5 3. Nc3 / Nc6 4. e3 {play 5}
# One Wing Angel / Sephiroth's Gambit (165): 4. d3 is punished by 4...b5 (fixed: "4. d3 b5").
# 5. Nxb5 loses to Qa5+ and Nb4 (169: "6. Be2" does not meet the check on the a5-e1 diagonal;
# the block 6. Bd2 is meant); after 5. cxb5 Black plays Nd4 (172), recaptures 6. Nxd4
# with cxd4 (175; the heading says Nxd5), meets 6. e3 with Nxb5 (178), 7. Nxb5 with Qa5+ and
# Qxb5 (181), and 7. Qb3 with Nd6 (prose of 183). Declined with 5. Qb3: 5...Nd4 (prose of 189).
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. c4 / c5 3. Nc3 / Nc6 4. d3 {know} / b5 {play 10} 5. Nxb5 {know} / Qa5+ {play 10} 6. Bd2 {know} / Nb4 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. c4 / c5 3. Nc3 / Nc6 4. d3 / b5 5. cxb5 {know} / Nd4 {play 10} 6. Nxd4 {know} / cxd4 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. c4 / c5 3. Nc3 / Nc6 4. d3 / b5 5. cxb5 / Nd4 6. e3 {know} / Nxb5 {play 10} 7. Nxb5 {know} / Qa5+ {play 10} 8. Bd2 {know} / Qxb5 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. c4 / c5 3. Nc3 / Nc6 4. d3 / b5 5. cxb5 / Nd4 6. e3 / Nxb5 7. Qb3 {know} / Nd6 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. c4 / c5 3. Nc3 / Nc6 4. d3 / b5 5. Qb3 {know} / Nd4 {play 10}

# --- Sarp's Counterattack (192-259): an aggressive Black alternative to 2...d5. White's main
# line is 3. c3 (t0_common_openings: "3. dxc5 doesn't win a pawn because of Qa5+ and Qxc5").
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / c5 {play 3} 3. c3 {play 10} / Qb6 {play 10}
# 3. dxc5: Queen's line (198, 201): Bd2 or Qd2 must answer the check; 4. Nc3 blunders to Ne4
# (204).
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / c5 3. dxc5 {know} / Qa5+ {play 10} 4. Bd2 {play 10} / Qxc5 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / c5 3. dxc5 / Qa5+ 4. Qd2 {play 5} / Qxc5 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / c5 3. dxc5 / Qa5+ 4. Nc3 {know} / Ne4 {play 10}
# The Bishop's line (207): solid for Black, "not a recommended line to play as white"; White
# must find 5. e3 (5. g3 loses to the Bxf2+ trap, 210).
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / c5 3. dxc5 / e6 {play 5} 4. Bd2 {play 10} / Bxc5 {play 10} 5. e3 {play 10} / Qb6 {play 10} 6. Qc1 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / c5 3. dxc5 / e6 4. Bd2 / Bxc5 5. g3 {know} / Bxf2+ {play 10} 6. Kxf2 {know} / Ne4+ {play 10} 7. Ke1 {know} / Qb6 {play 10}
# 3. c3 Qb6 mainline. 4. Qb3 forces Sarp's endgame (216, "balanced").
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / c5 3. c3 / Qb6 4. Qb3 {play 10} / Qxb3 {play 10} 5. axb3 {play 10}
# 4. e3: after 4...e6 Black gets the worse game (220); 4...Nc6 is Black's move, and then 5. Nbd2
# allows cxd4 with bad development for White (225).
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / c5 3. c3 / Qb6 4. e3 {play 5} / e6 {know} 5. Nbd2 {play 10} / cxd4 {know} 6. Nxd4 {play 10} / Nc6 {know} 7. Nc4 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / c5 3. c3 / Qb6 4. e3 / Nc6 {play 10} 5. Nbd2 {know} / cxd4 {play 10} 6. Nxd4 {know} / Nxd4 {play 10} 7. cxd4 {know}
# Dreamer's variation (228): sharp but equal with the forced moves.
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / c5 3. c3 / Qb6 4. e3 / Nc6 5. d5 {play 5} / Nb4 {play 10} 6. d6 {play 10} / e6 {play 10}
# Monte Carlo's Gambit (234-246): "with correct play, this should be better for black", so
# White's g4 is not for us; but each capture of the poisoned pawns loses material: Nbxd5 to g5
# (238), Nfxd5 to a3 (241: the line says g5, the prose and the board say a3: the queen hits d5
# and a3 the knight on b4), Nxg4 to Qa4 (244), and in the 6. d6 e6 7. g4 move order Black's
# only answer to 8. Qa4 is Nxf2 (246).
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / c5 3. c3 / Qb6 4. e3 / Nc6 5. d5 / Nb4 6. g4 {know} / Nbxd5 {know} 7. g5 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / c5 3. c3 / Qb6 4. e3 / Nc6 5. d5 / Nb4 6. g4 / Nfxd5 {know} 7. a3 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / c5 3. c3 / Qb6 4. e3 / Nc6 5. d5 / Nb4 6. g4 / Nxg4 {know} 7. Qa4 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / c5 3. c3 / Qb6 4. e3 / Nc6 5. d5 / Nb4 6. d6 / e6 7. g4 {know} / Nxg4 {know} 8. Qa4 {play 10} / Nxf2 {play 10}
# 5. Bc4 e6 (249; the line says "5. Bc5", which is no legal move: the heading and prose say Bc4).
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / c5 3. c3 / Qb6 4. e3 / Nc6 5. Bc4 {play 3} / e6 {play 10}
# 4. dxc5 Qxc5 5. Be3 (prose of 251: "white can stop some of black's counterplay ... equal"),
# 5...Qb5 and the endgame line (255; its header says Standard, the section is Turn Zero).
# 4...Qe6, the Senatus Gambit (258): with correct defence White is better.
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / c5 3. c3 / Qb6 4. dxc5 {play 3} / Qxc5 {play 10} 5. Be3 {play 10} / Qb5 {play 10} 6. Qb3 {play 10} / Qxb3 {play 10} 7. axb3 {play 10} / Nc6 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / c5 3. c3 / Qb6 4. dxc5 / Qe6 {know}

# --- Mongolian Attack 2. Nd4 (263-303; most lines' headers say Standard, the section is Turn
# Zero). "Very effective against the Classical f7 Defense" (2...d5); 2...e6 is "one of the most
# challenging lines against Mongolian", 2...c5 is tricky but playable.
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. Nd4 {know} / e6 {play 10} 3. e3 {know} / c5 {play 10} 4. Nf3 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. Nd4 / e6 3. c3 {play 5} / c5 {play 10} 4. Qb3 {play 10} / Qa5 {play 10}
# 2...d5 3. e3 c6 (266): 4. c4 (269) or the Bd3 Trojan trap (272): 4...Bg4 is met by the queen
# sac 5. Qxg4 (the guide's 274 has "Qxg5" and "Bf55"; its corrected Standard copy is 276, used
# here) and the bishop travel to f7.
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. Nd4 / d5 {know} 3. e3 {play 10} / c6 {know} 4. c4 {play 5}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. Nd4 / d5 3. e3 / c6 4. Bd3 {play 10} / Bg4 {know} 5. Qxg4 {play 10} / Nxg4 {know} 6. Bf5 {play 10} / Nf6 {know} 7. (0T7)Bf5>>x(0T5)f7~ (>L1) {play 10} / (1T5)Kxf7 {know} 8. (1T6)Qxg4 {play 10} / (1T6)Qc8 {know} 9. (1T7)Qxc8 {play 10} / (0T7)Qd8>x(1T7)c8 {know} 10. (1T8)Nf5 (0T8)Nf5 {play 10}
# 2...c5 (279): the Zigzag 3. Nf5 (282) is "a ridiculous approach": Black answers e6 and g6
# (285: "4. g6" is Black's reply to 4. e3 in the prose) or g6 at once (288).
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. Nd4 / c5 {play 3} 3. Nf5 {know} / e6 {play 10} 4. e3 {play 10} / g6 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. Nd4 / c5 3. Nf5 / g6 {play 5} 4. Nd6 {know} / exd6 {play 10}
# 3. c3, the f7-sac approach (291): taking the knight walks into the f7 sac and Nf5 softmate
# (293; fixed "(>1L)").
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. Nd4 / c5 3. c3 {play 5} / cxd4 {know} 4. Qb3 {play 10} / Qb6 {know} 5. Qc4 {play 10} / e6 {know} 6. (0T6)Qc4>>x(0T3)f7~ (>L1) {play 10} / (1T3)Kxf7 {know} 7. (1T4)Nf5 {play 10}

[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. e3 / c5 3. Bd3 {play 3}
# 2...e6 (349) is the most flexible; 3. c3 is "often not a good idea" and Black answers c5 or c6

# --- f7 sac defences (f7_sac_guide_notes.md, Lavamat's f7-sac guide; "in Turn Zero the same
# applies to both colours"). After 1. e3 or 1. c3 and 1...Nf6, White's queen and bishop routes
# to T1f7 run through b3, c4 and h5; Black closes them or covers the attacking queen.
# Against 2. Bb5: "main: 2...c6 3. c3 cxb5 4. Qb3 Qa5/Qb6/Qc7", or the queen on b3 is taken
# ("2. Bb5 a5 3. c3 a4 4. Qb3 axb3", "2...Nc6 3. c3 Na5 4. Qb3 Nxb3").

[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e3 / Nf6 2. Bb5 / c6 3. c3 / cxb5 4. Qb3 / Qa5 {play 5}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e3 / Nf6 2. Bb5 / c6 3. c3 / cxb5 4. Qb3 / Qc7 {play 5}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e3 / Nf6 2. Bb5 / a5 {play 3} 3. c3 {know} / a4 {play 10} 4. Qb3 {know} / axb3 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e3 / Nf6 2. Bb5 / Nc6 {play 3} 3. c3 {know} / Na5 {play 10} 4. Qb3 {know} / Nxb3 {play 10}

# Against 1. c3: "vs 2. Qa4 & 3. Qc4: 2...c6 3. Qc4 Qa5/Qb6/Qc7; vs 3. a3: 3...d5 4. Qb3

# --- London with 8. Ng5: docs/theory/lichess_study_london_ng5.pgn ("5d chess london Ng5", the
# user's Lichess study, PlankfullFire). Lichess only knows 2D chess, so each chapter is one board:
# the main chapters follow L0 from the position after 1. Nf3 Nf6 2. d4 d5 3. c3 c6 4. Bf4 Bf5
# 5. e3 e6 6. Bd3 Bxd3 7. Qxd3 Bd6 8. Ng5! (the "starting position" chapter), one chapter per
# Black 8th move (8...Bxf4, 8...Qe7, 8...Rf8, 8...g6) and sub-line; a travel is marked by the $138
# glyph and a blue arrow from the travelling piece, and each "travel idea N" chapter is the past
# board it lands on (the L0 board plus the arriving queen) with the play there; blue circled
# squares count turns for the travel's length. The study is White's repertoire: White's main
# and "!" moves are played, "!?" ones are sidelines (play 3), unmarked White alternatives and
# anything with ?, ?! or ?? are known; Black's moves are known unless marked ! or !!, except that
# after White's own ? or ?? Black's refutation is played. The lines are generated from the study,
# cut where the 5D rules refuse a move (8...Bxf4 ... 19. Qe3, after which every Black move is in
# check through time). Every played action was searched for 300 ms afterwards: none allows a
# forced mate (one White move that did, 23. Qxg6+ deep in the 8...Qe7 10. Nxf7 Kxf7 11. Qf1 Na6
# 12. f3?! line, is known and the line stops there). The "jurassic theory" chapters (Black's
# travels to the T0 board, not tied to a position of the main line), travel ideas 7 and 8 and
# idea 2.1's cross-timeline knight are not in the book.

# --- The Turn Zero main line in Standard (the guide's classical f7 defence and London; the f7
# routes are the same without the T0 board), then 4. Bg5 Bg4 5. e3 and the London study.
[Board "Standard"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 {play 10} / d5 {play 10} 3. c3 {play 10} / c6 {play 10} 4. Bf4 {play 10} / Bf5 {play 10}
[Board "Standard"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. d4 / d5 3. c3 / c6 4. Bg5 {play 5} / Bg4 {play 10}
# --- 4. Bg5 Bg4 5. e3 (expert feedback after a game Kairos lost as Black, 2026-10-17: "black is
# losing as soon as Bxf3 happens. And Ne4 is a move that is dangerous"; the game went 5...Ne4

# Travel idea 4 (Q>>g6): in the 8...g6 chapter ("solved 1-0") 11. Qc2! is played because

# ============================================================================ Guide v3.0
# Additions from the Theory Gang's "T0 Openings Guide" v3.0 (docs/theory/t0_opening_guide_v3.txt;
# the line numbers below are that file's) and the Magic Wall guide (docs/theory/magic_wall_guide.txt).
# v3.0 added: the Digolian and Atonal openings, the Magic Wall (1. e3 Nf6 2. b4), the asymmetrical
# English lines, and changes to c3/b3 against 2...e6 in the e3-versus-f7 section (already as
# not-recommended / main line in the sections above). Typos in the guide's lines fixed here:
# 6. Bb2 is 6. Bd2 (prose of 286-293), 4. Qb6 is 4. Qxb6 (303), (0T4)f5 is (0T4)f4 (312: the knight
# check is N>>f4*, footnote 1), "3. Nb4" is 3. Nb5 and Ne5/Nd7 need their knights named (519-558),
# 2. d5 is 2. d4 (738) and 4. Bc6 c6 is 4. Bxc6 bxc6 (730).

# --- Asymmetrical English (273-320): "players at higher levels have stopped playing
# symmetrically against the English" (c5 gives Black the more passive position), so 2...c6, the
# Jack the Ripper Variation (274: "the optimal way to play against the English"), and 2...e6.
# 3. Qb3 Qb6 4. Qxb6 axb6 trades queens "to disallow exiled kings" (275-278).
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. c4 / c6 {play 10} 3. Qb3 {play 10} / Qb6 {play 10} 4. Qxb6 {play 10} / axb6 {play 10}
# 5. d4 and 5. d3 both meet Na6 with Bd2, stopping Nb4 (285-296; the guide's "6. Bb2" is illegal).
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. c4 / c6 3. Qb3 / Qb6 4. Qxb6 / axb6 5. d4 {play 10} / Na6 {play 10} 6. Bd2 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. c4 / c6 3. Qb3 / Qb6 4. Qxb6 / axb6 5. d3 {play 5} / Na6 {play 10} 6. Bd2 {play 10}
# 5. Nc3, Ryan's variation (297-304): White lets Black go for Nb4-Nc2+ and walks the king to d1;
# "recommended only for advanced players".
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. c4 / c6 3. Qb3 / Qb6 4. Qxb6 / axb6 5. Nc3 {play 3} / Na6 {play 10} 6. a3 {play 10} / Nb4 {play 10} 7. Rb1 {play 10} / Nc2+ {play 10} 8. Kd1 {play 10}
# 3. e3 instead (279-284): "fine for White", the queen comes to b3 later.
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. c4 / c6 3. e3 {play 5} / Qb6 {play 10} 4. Qb3 {play 10}
# 2...e6 (305): Nf3 is enough defence on the kingside; 3. c5 is a "foolish attempt to f-sac"
# (307: Bxc5 stops Qb3's travel and the knight check N>>f4* cannot be stopped); 3. Nc3 is the
# main line (313-317: Black plays b6 and Bb4 to trade on c3).
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. c4 / e6 {play 5} 3. c5 {know} / Bxc5 {play 10} 4. Qb3 {know} / Bxf2+ {play 10} 5. Kxf2 {know} / (0T5)Nf6>>(0T4)f4* {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. c4 / e6 3. Nc3 {play 10} / b6 {play 10} 4. d4 {play 10} / Bb4 {play 10} 5. Bf4 {play 10} / Bxc3+ {play 10} 6. bxc3 {play 10}

# --- Digolian Attack, 2. Na3 (508-571): "an improved version of the Mongolian": two knights on the
# attack, "a landmine of traps at the cost of some tempo", recommended for intermediate and
# advanced players who like trappy positions. White's first knight goes a3-c4-e5.
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. Na3 {play 3} / c6 {know} 3. Nc4 {play 10} / d5 {know} 4. Nce5 {play 10}
# 4...Qb6 threatens Qxf2 and N>>f4#; 5. e3 answers it (520-521).
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. Na3 / c6 3. Nc4 / d5 4. Nce5 / Qb6 {know} 5. e3 {play 10}
# 4...Nbd7 and 4...Bf5 (523-531): Black stopped defending f5, so Nxf7 wins; the king may not
# retake because the knight travels to f5 with a softmate.
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. Na3 / c6 3. Nc4 / d5 4. Nce5 / Nbd7 {know} 5. Nxf7 {play 10} / Kxf7 {know} 6. (0T6)Nf3>>(0T5)f5~ {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. Na3 / c6 3. Nc4 / d5 4. Nce5 / Bf5 {know} 5. Nxf7 {play 10} / Kxf7 {know}
# 4...Be6 is passive (534-535: e3 and later Ng5); 4...Bg4 trades knights (537-545).
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. Na3 / c6 3. Nc4 / d5 4. Nce5 / Be6 {know} 5. e3 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. Na3 / c6 3. Nc4 / d5 4. Nce5 / Bg4 {know} 5. Nxg4 {play 10} / Nxg4 {know}
# 2...d5 (546-558): White goes for d4 through b5 (the guide's "Nb4" is Nb5); after 3...c6 4. Nbd4 the
# trap line against 4...Bg4 ends with Ne5>>x(0T8)e6 (554-558).
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. Na3 / d5 {play 5} 3. Nb5 {play 10} / c6 {know} 4. Nbd4 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. Na3 / d5 3. Nb5 / c6 4. Nbd4 / Bg4 {know} 5. e3 {play 10} / e6 {know} 6. h3 {play 10} / Bh5 {know} 7. Nxe6 {play 10} / fxe6 {know} 8. Ne5 {play 10} / Bxd1 {know} 9. Kxd1 {play 10} / Bd6 {know} 10. (0T10)Ne5>>x(0T8)e6 {play 10}
# 3. Nb5 c5, Digolian Denied (559-563), "one of the strongest responses": Black denies d4.
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. Na3 / d5 3. Nb5 / c5 {play 10} 4. c4 {play 10} / Nc6 {play 10}
# 2...e6 (564-571): "one of the best ways to play against the Digolian", solid and equal.
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. Na3 / e6 {play 10}

# --- Atonal Attack, 1. e4 (726-805): "one of the most chaotic and tricky openings", White plays for
# activity; "high risk, high reward". Black's safe reply is 1...Nf6 (defends against the f7 sac).
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e4 / d5 {know} 2. Qh5 {play 10} / Nf6 {know} 3. Bb5+ {play 10} / c6 {know} 4. Bxc6+ {play 10} / bxc6 {know} 5. (0T5)Qh5>>(0T3)f7 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e4 / Nf6 2. d4 {play 10} / d5 {play 10} 3. c3 {play 10} / c6 {play 10}
# 2. d4 Nxe4 loses to Qf3 (738-746: the guide's "2. d5" is 2. d4): after 3...Nf6 4. Qb3 is the f7 sac
# with no defence set up; 3...c6 allows a forced mate with piece sacrifices.
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e4 / Nf6 2. d4 / Nxe4 {know} 3. Qf3 {play 10} / Nf6 {know} 4. Qb3 {play 10}

# 3. c3 c6 (751-754, the mainline): 4. Bg5 Bg4 (768-774); 4. e5, the Hindemith variation, is losing
# for White after Bf5 and the bishop travel to f2 (755-767).
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e4 / Nf6 2. d4 / d5 3. c3 / c6 4. Bg5 {play 5} / Bg4 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e4 / Nf6 2. d4 / d5 3. c3 / c6 4. e5 {know} / Bf5 {play 10} 5. Be3 {play 10} / (0T5)Bf5>>(0T2)f2 {play 10}
# 2...e6 (777-800): White is in grave danger after 3. c3 Ng4 (the f2 sacrifice); 3. Bg5 is the
# defence. After 3...c5/c6 White plays b4 against Qa5#; after 3...h6 the bishop goes to h4.
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e4 / Nf6 2. d4 / e6 {play 5} 3. Bg5 {play 10} / c5 {play 5} 4. b4 {play 10}

# 2...c6 (801-805) is weak: 3. c3 closes the triagonal again.
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e4 / Nf6 2. d4 / c6 {know} 3. c3 {play 10}

# --- Magic Wall, 1. e3 Nf6 2. b4 (648-662; the "e3 b4 Bb2" anti-f-sac guide): White gets quick
# development with an open queen and bishops. Black targets e3 and f2. 2...e6 is the main line
# ("do not take the b4 pawn": losing a tempo can be devastating).
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e3 / Nf6 2. b4 {play 3} / e6 {play 10} 3. Bb2 {play 10} / d5 {play 10}
# 2...d5 is "playable but not recommended" (653); 3. Bb2 c6? 4. Bxf6 h5 is the guide's sample of
# the mistake (Magic Wall guide: "short sample of 2nd d5 versions").
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e3 / Nf6 2. b4 / d5 {play 2} 3. Bb2 {play 10} / c6 {know} 4. Bxf6 {play 10} / h5 {know} 5. Bxe7 {play 10} / Qxe7 {know} 6. Qf3 {play 10} / f5 {know} 7. Qg3 {play 10} / g5 {know} 8. Nf3 {play 10}
# 2...Ng4 3. Qxg4 f5 4. Qxf5 g6 (Magic Wall guide, "2. b4 / Ng4"): White wins the knight and
# keeps the safer king.
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e3 / Nf6 2. b4 / Ng4 {know} 3. Qxg4 {play 10} / f5 {know} 4. Qxf5 {play 10} / g6 {know}
# 3. Bb2: the answers to the other third moves (Magic Wall guide).
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e3 / Nf6 2. b4 / e6 3. Bb2 / Nd5 {know} 4. Qh5 {play 10} / g6 {know} 5. Qxd5 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e3 / Nf6 2. b4 / e6 3. Bb2 / Ne4 {know} 4. Qh5 {play 10} / g6 {know} 5. Qf3 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e3 / Nf6 2. b4 / e6 3. Bb2 / g6 {know} 4. Qf3 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e3 / Nf6 2. b4 / e6 3. Bb2 / h5 {know} 4. Qf3 {play 10} / Qe7 {know} 5. a3 {play 10} / Ng4 {know} 6. Qg3 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e3 / Nf6 2. b4 / e6 3. Bb2 / Ng4 {know} 4. Qxg4 {play 10} / f5 {know}
# 3. c3 allows ...Ng4 (the knight checks on e3 and Qh4 follows); other second moves are
# playable for Black (c5, a5, h5: "the most short-term safety").
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e3 / Nf6 2. b4 / e6 3. c3 {play 3} / Ng4 {know}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e3 / Nf6 2. b4 / c5 {play 2}

[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. e3 / Nf6 2. b4 / h5 {play 2} 3. c3 {play 10}

# ============================================================================ Defended Pawn
# Standard - Defended Pawn: queens on b1/b8 and knights on d1/d8 start the game (so these lines only
# ever match that variant). Source: RyanFourLC's Lichess study "5D Chess Defended Pawn Ryan's
# Opening" (docs/theory/lichess_study_defended_pawn_ryan.pgn). The study is Black's repertoire:
# "the setup" is Nf6, Ne6, g6, Bg7, O-O (all the squares around the king are hard to reach by
# travels and defended), and the chapters show how to meet White's attempts. Every chapter is one
# board from the start position. The author later retired the opening (first chapter: White's
# h4-h5 plan against g6 is good "regardless of whether black has castled", "never play g6 or f6"),
# so Black's g6 is only known theory here, not played, and the long Black repertoire after it (the
# chapters on 5. Nc3, 7. Bxf6, 7. Bc3 ...) is NOT in the book (kept in the study file only). What
# is used: the flexible first moves that stay sound (1. c4 Nf6 2. Nf3 Ne6; 1. b4 "barely different"),
# the replies to 3. d4 and to the too-slow 1...c5, and the one plan White is meant to play against
# g6: 5. h4.
# --- 1. c4 (chapter "1. c4"): 1...Nf6 "always goes to f6, so this is the most flexible move";
# 1...Ne6 undefends f7, 1...g6 is the most inflexible, 1...c5 and 1...b5 are too slow.
[Board "Standard - Defended Pawn"] [Mode "5D"] 1. c4 {play 10} / Nf6 {play 10} 2. Nf3 {play 10} / Ne6 {play 10} 3. b3 {play 10} / g6 {know} 4. Bb2 {play 10} / Bg7 {know} 5. h4 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Defended Pawn"] [Mode "5D"] 1. c4 / Ne6 {know}
[Board "Standard - Defended Pawn"] [Mode "5D"] 1. c4 / g6 {know}
[Board "Standard - Defended Pawn"] [Mode "5D"] 1. c4 / b5 {know}
# 2...g6 "has no purpose yet": the fianchetto is committed to as late as possible.
[Board "Standard - Defended Pawn"] [Mode "5D"] 1. c4 / Nf6 2. Nf3 / g6 {know}
# 1...c5 is too slow (optional chapter "Too Slow"): White gets a comfortable game; 6. Nd5 stops the
# castling (Nxe7 is mate) and the trades set up a bishop travel onto e7.
[Board "Standard - Defended Pawn"] [Mode "5D"] 1. c4 / c5 {know} 2. Nf3 {play 10} / Nf6 {know} 3. b3 {play 10} / Ne6 {know} 4. Bb2 {play 10} / g6 {know} 5. Nc3 {play 10} / Bg7 {know} 6. Nd5 {play 10} / Nxd5 {know} 7. cxd5 {play 10} / Nd4 {know} 8. e3 {play 10} / Nxf3+ {know} 9. gxf3 {play 10} / Bxb2 {know} 10. Qxb2 {play 10} / f6 {know} 11. e4 {play 10} / Qe5 {know} 12. Be2 {play 10}
# 1. b4 (chapter "1. b4") "will either transpose to 1. c4 or allow you to reach the setup".
[Board "Standard - Defended Pawn"] [Mode "5D"] 1. b4 {play 3} / Nf6 {play 10} 2. Nf3 {play 10} / Ne6 {play 10} 3. Bb2 {play 10} / g6 {know} 4. Nc3 {know} / Bg7 {know}
# 3. d4 (chapter "1. c4 with 3. d4"): 3...b5 attacks c4 (d5 is met by bxc4 and Qb4); 4. b3 Qb6
# and Black wins a pawn after 5. Bd2; 3...c5 and 4...bxc4 are also fine.
[Board "Standard - Defended Pawn"] [Mode "5D"] 1. c4 / Nf6 2. Nf3 / Ne6 3. d4 {know} / b5 {play 10} 4. b3 {know} / Qb6 {play 10} 5. Bd2 {know}
[Board "Standard - Defended Pawn"] [Mode "5D"] 1. c4 / Nf6 2. Nf3 / Ne6 3. d4 / c5 {play 3}
[Board "Standard - Defended Pawn"] [Mode "5D"] 1. c4 / Nf6 2. Nf3 / Ne6 3. d4 / b5 4. b3 / bxc4 {play 3}

# position reached there (an `N` line in the book file; `Book::opening_name`). The names are the

# Pawn study); a game's opening is the last name found along it. The same names are given on
# the Standard board for the lines that make sense there.

# ============================================================================ Contributed lines (2026-10)
# Lines sent by T0 players. Most transpose to known theory through another move order (the book
# is keyed by the boards in play, so they share positions); these add the lines past it. A first
# move the book did not play before (1. d4, 1...e6) is only known; the rest is played.
# --- 1. d4 Nf6 2. Bf4 (London move order): d5 3. c3 c6 reaches the Classical f7 Defense; 2...c5 3. b4.
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. d4 {know} / Nf6 {play 10} 2. Bf4 {play 5} / d5 {play 10} 3. c3 {play 5} / c6 {play 10}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. d4 / Nf6 2. Bf4 / c5 {play 3} 3. b4 {play 3}
# --- Symmetrical English 1. Nf3 Nf6 2. c4 c5: 3. b3, 3. e3, 3. g3 with the replies g6, Nc6, e6, b6.
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. c4 / c5 3. b3 {play 3}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. c4 / c5 3. e3 {play 3}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. c4 / c5 3. g3 {play 3} / g6 {play 3}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. c4 / c5 3. g3 / Nc6 {play 3}
[Board "Standard - Turn Zero"] [Mode "5D"] 1. Nf3 / Nf6 2. c4 / c5 3. g3 / e6 {play 3}
